### TC-64

````text
model=a3b-coder

prompt=Write a review of the movie 'The Matrix'. Output ONLY valid JSON matching this schema — no extra text.

Schema:

{
  "type": "object",
  "properties": {
    "title": {
      "type": "string"
    },
    "year": {
      "type": "integer"
    },
    "rating": {
      "type": "number",
      "minimum": 0,
      "maximum": 10
    },
    "genre": {
      "type": "string",
      "enum": [
        "action",
        "comedy",
        "drama",
        "horror",
        "sci-fi",
        "thriller"
      ]
    },
    "summary": {
      "type": "string"
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "title",
    "year",
    "rating",
    "genre",
    "summary"
  ],
  "additionalProperties": false
}
```

available_tools=(none)

  "rating": 9.5,

}

}
```

- title: string -> "The Matrix"
- year: integer -> 1999
- rating: number, min 0, max 10 -> 9.5
- genre: string, enum -> "sci-fi"
- summary: string -> valid
- required: all present
- additionalProperties: false -> only these keys

Matches perfectly. Output only JSON.
Done. 
I will generate exactly the JSON string.
No extra text.
Proceed.

Check enum values: "action", "comedy", "drama", "horror", "sci-fi", "thriller". Note the typo "horror" in the prompt, but I'll use "sci-fi" which is correct.
All good.
Output matches exactly.
Proceeds.
````

### TC-88

```text
model=a3b-coder
scenario=TC-88 Preserved Reasoning Across Follow-Ups
prompt=Privately construct three distinct 20-digit numbers with no leading zero. Their digit sums must be 73, 91, and 109 in that order. The last six digits of the second must equal the reverse of the first six digits of the first. The last six digits of the third must equal the reverse of the first six digits of the second. Verify every length, sum, and reversal in your reasoning. Do not use tools. Reply with only the first number and nothing else.

assistant=starting
available_tools=(none)
assistant_turn_1=[tool_calls_only]
assistant_reasoning_1=Here's a thinking process:

1.  **Analyze User Request:**
   - Construct three distinct 20-digit numbers.
   - No leading zeros.
   - Digit sums must be 73, 91, and 109 in that order.
   - Constraint 1: Last 6 digits of the second number = reverse of first 6 digits of the first number.
   - Constraint 2: Last 6 digits of the third number = reverse of first 6 digits of the second number.
   - Verify every length, sum, and reversal in reasoning.
   - Do not use tools.
   - Reply with *only* the first number and nothing else.

2.  **Deconstruct Constraints:**
   - Let $N_1, N_2, N_3$ be the three numbers.
   - Each has exactly 20 digits.
   - $S(N_1) = 73$, $S(N_2) = 91$, $S(N_3) = 109$.
   - Let $N_1 = A_1 B_1$, where $A_1$ is the first 6 digits, $B_1$ is the last 14 digits.
   - Let $N_2 = A_2 B_2$, where $A_2$ is the first 6 digits, $B_2$ is the last 14 digits.
   - Let $N_3 = A_3 B_3$, where $A_3$ is the first 6 digits, $B_3$ is the last 14 digits.
   - Constraint: $B_2 = \text{reverse}(A_1)$.
   - Constraint: $B_3 = \text{reverse}(A_2)$.
   - So $N_1 = A_1 \cdot 10^{14} + \text{reverse}(A_1) \cdot 10^0$? Wait, $B_2$ is the last 6 digits of $N_2$, so $B_2$ is a 6-digit number. $B_2 = \text{reverse}(A_1)$.
   - $B_3$ is the last 6 digits of $N_3$, so $B_3 = \text{reverse}(A_2)$.
   - Let's denote digits explicitly to avoid confusion.
   - $N_1 = d_{1,1} d_{1,2} d_{1,3} d_{1,4} d_{1,5} d_{1,6} \dots d_{1,20}$
   - $N_2 = d_{2,1} d_{2,2} d_{2,3} d_{2,4} d_{2,5} d_{2,6} \dots d_{2,20}$
   - $N_3 = d_{3,1} d_{3,2} d_{3,3} d_{3,4} d_{3,5} d_{3,6} \dots d_{3,20}$
   - Constraint: $d_{2,15} d_{2,16} d_{2,17} d_{2,18} d_{2,19} d_{2,20} = \text{reverse}(d_{1,1} d_{1,2} d_{1,3} d_{1,4} d_{1,5} d_{1,6})$
     So $d_{2,15} = d_{1,6}, d_{2,16} = d_{1,5}, d_{2,17} = d_{1,4}, d_{2,18} = d_{1,3}, d_{2,19} = d_{1,2}, d_{2,20} = d_{1,1}$.
   - Constraint: $d_{3,15} d_{3,16} d_{3,17} d_{3,18} d_{3,19} d_{3,20} = \text{reverse}(d_{2,1} d_{2,2} d_{2,3} d_{2,4} d_{2,5} d_{2,6})$
     So $d_{3,15} = d_{2,6}, d_{3,16} = d_{2,5}, d_{3,17} = d_{2,4}, d_{3,18} = d_{2,3}, d_{3,19} = d_{2,2}, d_{3,20} = d_{2,1}$.
   - Digit sums:
     $S(N_1) = \sum_{i=1}^6 d_{1,i} + \sum_{i=7}^{14} d_{1,i} + \sum_{i=15}^{20} d_{1,i} = 73$
     Note: $\sum_{i=15}^{20} d_{1,i} = \sum_{i=1}^6 d_{1,i}$ because the last 6 digits are just the first 6 digits reversed. Wait, reversal doesn't change the sum of digits. So sum of last 6 digits of $N_1$ = sum of first 6 digits of $N_1$.
     Let $S_1 = \sum_{i=1}^6 d_{1,i}$. Then sum of last 6 digits of $N_1$ is also $S_1$.
     So $S(N_1) = 2 S_1 + \sum_{i=7}^{14} d_{1,i} = 73$.
     Similarly, $S(N_2) = 2 S_2 + \sum_{i=7}^{14} d_{2,i} = 91$, where $S_2 = \sum_{i=1}^6 d_{2,i}$.
     $S(N_3) = 2 S_3 + \sum_{i=7}^{14} d_{3,i} = 109$, where $S_3 = \sum_{i=1}^6 d_{3,i}$.
   - Also, $S_2 = \sum_{i=1}^6 d_{2,i}$. The last 6 digits of $N_2$ are the reverse of the first 6 digits of $N_1$, so their sum is $S_1$. But $S_2$ is the sum of the *first* 6 digits of $N_2$. There's no direct constraint linking $S_1$ and $S_2$ except through the digits themselves. Wait, the constraint is about the *values* of the digits, not just sums.
   - Let's pick $A_1$ (first 6 digits of $N_1$) such that $S_1$ is reasonable. Max sum for 6 digits is $9 \times 6 = 54$. Min sum is 1 (since no leading zero).
   - $2 S_1 + \text{sum of middle 8 digits} = 73$.
     Middle 8 digits sum max = $9 \times 8 = 72$. Min = 0.
     So $2 S_1$ can range from $73 - 72 = 1$ to $73 - 0 = 73$. But $S_1 \le 54$. So $2 S_1 \le 108$. This is fine.
     Let's pick $S_1 = 20$. Then middle 8 digits sum = $73 - 40 = 33$.
     Let's pick $S_2 = 30$. Then middle 8 digits sum = $91 - 60 = 31$.
     Let's pick $S_3 = 40$. Then middle 8 digits sum = $109 - 80 = 29$.
```
